A sequential dual-backward decomposes the task and anchor gradients; for each transformer layer, AEGIS applies a single Gram-Schmidt orthogonal projection that bends the task gradient away from the destructive direction while preserving its constructive content. The projection sheds less than 1% of gradient energy on average, yet eliminates the cumulative activation drift that drives severe forgetting.

</details>


### [161] [Prototype-Grounded Concept Models for Verifiable Concept Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16076)
*Stefano Colamonaco,David Debot,Pietro Barbiero,Giuseppe Marra*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) aim to improve interpretability in Deep Learning by structuring predictions through human-understandable concepts, but they provide no way to verify whether learned concepts align with the human's intended meaning, hurting interpretability. We introduce Prototype-Grounded Concept Models (PGCMs), which ground concepts in learned visual prototypes: image parts that serve as explicit evidence for the concepts. This grounding enables direct inspection of concept semantics and supports targeted human intervention at the prototype level to correct misalignments. Empirically, PGCMs match the predictive performance of state-of-the-art CBMs while substantially improving transparency, interpretability, and intervenability.

</details>


### [162] [The Harder Path: Last Iterate Convergence for Uncoupled Learning in Zero-Sum Games with Bandit Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16087)
*Côme Fiegel,Pierre Ménard,Tadashi Kozuno,Michal Valko,Vianney Perchet*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: We study the problem of learning in zero-sum matrix games with repeated play and bandit feedback. Specifically, we focus on developing uncoupled algorithms that guarantee, without communication between players, the convergence of the last-iterate to a Nash equilibrium. Although the non-bandit case has been studied extensively, this setting has only been explored recently, with a bound of $\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/8})$ on the exploitability gap. We show that, for uncoupled algorithms, guaranteeing convergence of the policy profiles to a Nash equilibrium is detrimental to the performance, with the best attainable rate being $Ω(T^{-1/4})$ in contrast to the usual $Ω(T^{-1/2})$ rate for convergence of the average iterates. We then propose two algorithms that achieve this optimal rate up to constant and logarithmic factors. The first algorithm leverages a straightforward trade-off between exploration and exploitation, while the second employs a regularization technique based on a two-step mirror descent approach.

</details>


### [163] [Sample Complexity Bounds for Stochastic Shortest Path with a Generative Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16111)
*Jean Tarbouriech,Matteo Pirotta,Michal Valko,Alessandro Lazaric*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本文研究了随机最短路径(SSP)问题中学习ε-最优策略的样本复杂度。作者证明了存在下界Ω(SAB_star³/(c_min ε²))，并惊人地发现当c_min=0时问题可能不可学习，表明SSP学习严格难于有限时域和折扣设置。同时给出了匹配该下界的算法。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 理解随机最短路径(SSP)问题中策略学习的样本复杂度下限，以及c_min=0这一特殊边界情况下的可学习性问题。

Method: 1) 通过构造最坏情况SSP实例证明样本复杂度下界；2) 设计匹配该下界的算法（一般情况和c_min=0但有界访问时间的特殊情况的算法）。

Result: 证明了下界Ω(SAB_star³/(c_min ε²))；发现当c_min=0时SSP问题可能不可学习；给出了在一般情况和有界访问时间条件下匹配该下界的算法。

Conclusion: SSP学习的样本复杂度下界为Ω(SAB_star³/(c_min ε²))；当最小成本c_min=0时问题不可学习，揭示了SSP严格难于有限时域和折扣设置；算法可在对数因子内匹配该下界。

Abstract: We study the sample complexity of learning an $ε$-optimal policy in the Stochastic Shortest Path (SSP) problem. We first derive sample complexity bounds when the learner has access to a generative model. We show that there exists a worst-case SSP instance with $S$ states, $A$ actions, minimum cost $c_{\min}$, and maximum expected cost of the optimal policy over all states $B_{\star}$, where any algorithm requires at least $Ω(SAB_{\star}^3/(c_{\min}ε^2))$ samples to return an $ε$-optimal policy with high probability. Surprisingly, this implies that whenever $c_{\min} = 0$ an SSP problem may not be learnable, thus revealing that learning in SSPs is strictly harder than in the finite-horizon and discounted settings. We complement this lower bound with an algorithm that matches it, up to logarithmic factors, in the general case, and an algorithm that matches it up to logarithmic factors even when $c_{\min} = 0$, but only under the condition that the optimal policy has a bounded hitting time to the goal state.

</details>


### [164] [SCRIPT: Implementing an Intelligent Tutoring System for Programming in a German University Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16117)
*Alina Deriyeva,Jesper Dannath,Benjamin Paassen*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本文描述了一个面向Python编程的新型智能辅导系统（ITS）的设计与开发现状，旨在为编程教育提供可扩展、适应性强的教学与研究平台，支持通过大语言模型等机制插入提示功能，同时满足德国及欧盟的法规合规要求（GDPR、欧盟AI法案、DFG伦理框架）。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 当前智能辅导系统存在三大局限：①对Python语言支持不足；②多聚焦于入门级编程；③未充分利用生成式模型的最新进展。此外，德国严格的法规环境对教育技术系统提出了额外的合规挑战。

Method: 本文采用系统描述性方法，阐述了ITS的架构设计与当前开发状态，并讨论了未来扩展方向。系统设计强调模块化与可插拔性，允许集成LLM等新型提示机制。

Result: 本文为一项进展性工作（design paper），主要产出为系统架构描述与开发路线图，涵盖系统的适应性设计、教学与研究双重功能定位，以及法规合规性考量。

Conclusion: 该ITS有望填补Python编程智能辅导领域的空白，其模块化设计为后续集成先进生成模型提供了基础，同时合规性框架确保了系统在欧盟法规环境下的可行性。未来工作将聚焦于提示机制的实证评估与系统优化。

Abstract: Practice and extensive exercises are essential in programming education. Intelligent tutoring systems (ITSs) are a viable option to provide individualized hints and advice to programming students even when human tutors are not available. However, prior ITS for programming rarely support the Python programming language, mostly focus on introductory programming, and rarely take recent developments in generative models into account. We aim to establish a novel ITS for Python programming that is highly adaptable, serves both as a teaching and research platform, provides interfaces to plug in hint mechanisms (e.g.\ via large language models), and works inside the particularly challenging regulatory environment of Germany, that is, conforming to the European data protection regulation, the European AI act, and ethical framework of the German Research Foundation. In this paper, we present the description of the current state of the ITS along with future development directions, as well as discuss the challenges and opportunities for improving the system.

</details>


### [165] [Univariate Channel Fusion for Multivariate Time Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16119)
*Fernando Moro,Vinicius M. A. Souza*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: This paper proposes Univariate Channel Fusion (UCF), a lightweight method that transforms multivariate time series into univariate representation using simple fusion strategies (mean, median, DTW barycenter). This allows any univariate classifier to be applied to MTSC problems, achieving competitive accuracy with significantly reduced computational cost across five diverse application domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MTSC approaches, especially deep learning models, require high computational resources, making them unsuitable for real-time applications or deployment on low-cost hardware (IoT devices, wearable systems).

Method: UCF transforms multivariate time series into univariate representation through channel fusion strategies (mean, median, or DTW barycenter), enabling the use of any univariate time series classifier.

Result: UCF often outperforms baseline methods and state-of-the-art MTSC algorithms while achieving substantial computational efficiency gains, particularly effective in problems with high inter-channel correlation.

Conclusion: UCF provides a computationally lightweight and flexible solution for MTSC, with strong performance across diverse domains and particular effectiveness for problems with high inter-channel correlation.

Abstract: Multivariate time series classification (MTSC) plays a crucial role in various domains, including biomedical signal analysis and motion monitoring. However, existing approaches, particularly deep learning models, often require high computational resources, making them unsuitable for real-time applications or deployment on low-cost hardware, such as IoT devices and wearable systems. In this paper, we propose the Univariate Channel Fusion (UCF) method to deal with MTSC efficiently. UCF transforms multivariate time series into a univariate representation through simple channel fusion strategies such as the mean, median, or dynamic time warping barycenter. This transformation enables the use of any classifier originally designed for univariate time series, providing a flexible and computationally lightweight alternative to complex models. We evaluate UCF in five case studies covering diverse application domains, including chemical monitoring, brain-computer interfaces, and human activity analysis. The results demonstrate that UCF often outperforms baseline methods and state-of-the-art algorithms tailored for MTSC, while achieving substantial gains in computational efficiency, being particularly effective in problems with high inter-channel correlation.

</details>


### [166] [JumpLoRA: Sparse Adapters for Continual Learning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16171)
*Alexandra Dragomir,Ioana Pintilie,Antonio Barbalau,Marius Dragoi,Florin Brad,Cristian Daniel Paduraru,Alexandru Tifrea,Elena Burceanu,Radu Tudor Ionescu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: JumpLoRA introduces adaptive sparsity in LoRA blocks via JumpReLU gating to achieve dynamic parameter isolation, effectively reducing task interference in LLM continual learning while maintaining compatibility with existing LoRA-based CL methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing adapter-based continual learning methods for LLMs suffer from task interference when imposing constraints on new adapters, whether through subspace or coordinate-wise approaches.

Method: JumpLoRA employs JumpReLU gating to adaptively induce sparsity in LoRA blocks, enabling dynamic parameter isolation that mitigates task interference.

Result: JumpLoRA significantly outperforms the state-of-the-art method ELLA and substantially boosts the performance of IncLoRA in continual learning scenarios.

Conclusion: JumpLoRA achieves dynamic parameter isolation through sparsity induction, demonstrating high modularity and compatibility with existing LoRA-based continual learning methods, offering a promising approach for efficient LLM adaptation.

Abstract: Adapter-based methods have become a cost-effective approach to continual learning (CL) for Large Language Models (LLMs), by sequentially learning a low-rank update matrix for each task. To mitigate catastrophic forgetting, state-of-the-art approaches impose constraints on new adapters with respect to the previous ones, by targeting either subspace or coordinate-wise interference. In this paper, we propose JumpLoRA, a novel framework to adaptively induce sparsity in the Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) blocks through the use of JumpReLU gating. The method achieves dynamic parameter isolation, which helps prevent task interference. We demonstrate that our method is highly modular and compatible with LoRA-based CL approaches. Specifically, it significantly boosts the performance of IncLoRA and outperforms the leading state-of-the-art CL method, ELLA.

</details>


### [167] [Tabular foundation models for in-context prediction of molecular properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16123)
*Karim K. Ben Hicham,Jan G. Rittig,Martin Grohe,Alexander Mitsos*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本文评估了表格基础模型（TFMs）在低至中等数据条件下预测分子性质的性能，发现通过上下文学习而非任务特定微调的方式，TFMs结合CheMeleon嵌入在MoleculeACE基准上达到100%胜率，同时显著降低计算成本，表明分子表示质量是决定TFM性能的关键因素。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world molecular property prediction applications face limitations due to small datasets, while existing molecular foundation models require task-specific fine-tuning, demand machine learning expertise, and often fail to outperform classical baselines.

Method: Evaluate tabular foundation models (TFMs) in the low- to medium-data regime across pharmaceutical benchmarks and chemical engineering datasets, comparing frozen molecular foundation model representations with classical descriptors and fingerprints, using in-context learning instead of task-specific fine-tuning.

Result: TFMs with CheMeleon embeddings achieve up to 100% win rates on 30 MoleculeACE tasks; RDKit2d and Mordred descriptors provide strong alternatives; molecular foundation model embeddings and 2D descriptor sets substantially outperform classic molecular fingerprints on many tasks.

Conclusion: In-context learning with TFMs provides a highly accurate and cost-efficient alternative for molecular property prediction, with TFMs + CheMeleon achieving up to 100% win rates on MoleculeACE benchmarks, while molecular representation quality is identified as a key determinant of TFM performance.

Abstract: Accurate molecular property prediction is central to drug discovery, catalysis, and process design, yet real-world applications are often limited by small datasets. Molecular foundation models provide a promising direction by learning transferable molecular representations; however, they typically involve task-specific fine-tuning, require machine learning expertise, and often fail to outperform classical baselines. Tabular foundation models (TFMs) offer a fundamentally different paradigm: they perform predictions through in-context learning, enabling inference without task-specific training. Here, we evaluate TFMs in the low- to medium-data regime across both standardized pharmaceutical benchmarks and chemical engineering datasets. We evaluate both frozen molecular foundation model representations, as well as classical descriptors and fingerprints. Across the benchmarks, the approach shows excellent predictive performance while reducing computational cost, compared to fine-tuning, with these advantages also transferring to practical engineering data settings. In particular, combining TFMs with CheMeleon embeddings yields up to 100\% win rates on 30 MoleculeACE tasks, while compact RDKit2d and Mordred descriptors provide strong descriptor-based alternatives. Molecular representation emerges as a key determinant in TFM performance, with molecular foundation model embeddings and 2D descriptor sets both providing substantial gains over classic molecular fingerprints on many tasks. These results suggest that in-context learning with TFMs provides a highly accurate and cost-efficient alternative for property prediction in practical applications.

</details>


### [168] [Detecting and Suppressing Reward Hacking with Gradient Fingerprints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16242)
*Songtao Wang,Quang Hieu Pham,Fangcong Yin,Xinpeng Wang,Jocelyn Qiaochu Chen,Greg Durrett,Xi Ye*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GRIFT通过分析链式思考的梯度特征来检测强化学习中的奖励黑客行为，相比现有文本监控方法，在数学、代码和逻辑推理任务中检测准确率提升超过25%，可有效减少奖励黑客并提升任务表现。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RLVR optimizes for outcome rewards without constraints on intermediate reasoning, leaving training susceptible to reward hacking where models exploit loopholes in the reward function to achieve high scores without solving the intended task. These reward-hacking behaviors are often implicit as the intermediate CoT may appear plausible on the surface, limiting text-based monitoring effectiveness.

Method: GRIFT computes gradients of the chain-of-thought conditioned on the prompt and compresses them into a compact representation, which is then used to assess whether the CoT reflects reward hacking behavior.

Result: Across verifiable reasoning benchmarks spanning math, code, and logical reasoning, GRIFT substantially outperforms strong baselines (CoT Monitor and TRACE), achieving over 25% relative improvement in detecting reward hacking behavior. Integration into rejection fine-tuning pipeline reduces reward hacking and improves performance on the true task objective.

Conclusion: GRIFT represents a promising direction for leveraging gradient-level representations to assess the quality of chain-of-thought reasoning traces, with potential for integration into rejection fine-tuning pipelines to improve model performance on true task objectives.

Abstract: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) typically optimizes for outcome rewards without imposing constraints on intermediate reasoning. This leaves training susceptible to reward hacking, where models exploit loopholes (e.g., spurious patterns in training data) in the reward function to achieve high scores without solving the intended task. These reward-hacking behaviors are often implicit, as the intermediate chain-of-thought (CoT) may appear plausible on the surface, limiting the effectiveness of purely text-based monitoring. We propose Gradient Fingerprint (GRIFT), a method for detecting reward hacking using models' internal computations. Given a prompt and a model-generated CoT, GRIFT computes gradients of the CoT conditioned on the prompt and compresses them into a compact representation, which is then used to assess whether the CoT reflects reward hacking behavior. Across verifiable reasoning benchmarks spanning math, code, and logical reasoning, GRIFT substantially outperforms strong baselines, including CoT Monitor and TRACE, achieving over 25% relative improvement in detecting reward hacking behavior. Moreover, integrating GRIFT into the rejection fine-tuning pipeline for reasoning tasks reduces reward hacking and improves performance on the true task objective. Our results highlight a promising direction of leveraging gradient level representations for assessing the quality of CoT reasoning traces. Our code is available at: https://github.com/songtao-x/reward_hack.

</details>


### [169] [Sketching the Readout of Large Language Models for Scalable Data Attribution and Valuation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16197)
*Yide Ran,Jianwen Xie,Minghui Wang,Wenjin Zheng,Denghui Zhang,Chuan Li,Zhaozhuo Xu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: RISE是一种用于大语言模型数据归因和估值的新型可扩展方法，通过聚焦输出层的双通道表示（词汇残差通道RH和语义投影误差通道GH）实现高效压缩，解决了现有梯度方法在LLM上的可扩展性问题，在OLMo和Pythia模型族上验证，索引存储减少112倍，支持32B参数模型，并在后门检测、领域分离和高质量数据选择三个任务上验证有效。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing gradient-based methods for data attribution and valuation suffer from scalability challenges on large LLMs, making them computationally expensive and memory-infeasible for modern large-scale models.

Method: RISE focuses on influence hotspots at the output layer where gradient signals concentrate, using a dual-channel representation: lexical residual channel (RH) and semantic projected-error channel (GH). CountSketch projections are applied to achieve strong compression while maintaining accurate attribution. This design avoids computing and indexing gradients across the entire LLM.

Result: RISE reduces index storage by up to 112× compared to RapidIn and scales to 32B parameter LLMs where gradient-based baselines (RapidIn, ZO-Inf) become memory-infeasible. Validated on three tasks: Howdy backdoor detection, Finance-Medical domain separation, and Brain Rot data selection, with closed-loop studies showing consistent downstream improvements.

Conclusion: RISE provides a practical and scalable primitive for influence analysis and training-data selection in modern large language models, with the dual-channel approach enabling both retrospective attribution and prospective valuation across diverse downstream tasks.

Abstract: Data attribution and valuation are critical for understanding data-model synergy for Large Language Models (LLMs), yet existing gradient-based methods suffer from scalability challenges on LLMs. Inspired by human cognition, where decision making relies on a focused readout of relevant memories rather than replaying all pathways, we introduce RISE (Readout Influence Sketching Estimator). Instead of computing and indexing gradients across the entire LLM, RISE focuses on influence hotspots at the output layer, where influence signals concentrate, and the gradient admits a decomposed outer-product form. This enables a dual-channel representation combining a lexical residual channel (RH) and a semantic projected-error channel (GH). Applying CountSketch projections to these channels achieves strong compression while maintaining accurate attribution. Across the OLMo (1B-32B) and Pythia (14M-6.9B) families, RISE reduces index storage by up to 112$\times$ compared to RapidIn and scales to 32B parameters LLM, where gradient-based baselines such as RapidIn and ZO-Inf become memory-infeasible. We evaluate RISE on two paradigms: (1) retrospective attribution, retrieving influential training examples for specific predictions, and (2) prospective valuation, scoring candidate data utility zero-shot. We validate RISE on three tasks: Howdy backdoor data detection, Finance-Medical domain separation, and Brain Rot high-quality data selection. In a closed-loop Brain Rot study, continued pretraining on RISE-selected data yields consistent downstream improvements. Overall, RISE provides a practical and scalable primitive for influence analysis and training-data selection in modern large language models.

</details>


### [170] [OT on the Map: Quantifying Domain Shifts in Geographic Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16220)
*Haoran Zhang,Livia Betti,Konstantin Klemmer,Esther Rolf,David Alvarez-Melis*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GeoSpOT是一种利用地理信息和最优传输方法计算地理空间域间距离的策略，可有效预测跨域迁移难度，即使在下游任务未知或无任务数据时也能估计域外性能。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 地理数据分布不均及跨区域分布偏移导致域外泛化问题普遍存在，但目前缺乏确定跨区域适应成功与否的原则性方法。

Method: 提出GeoSpOT策略，结合地理信息与最优传输(Optimal Transport)方法计算地理空间域间距离。

Result: GeoSpOT距离可有效预测跨域迁移难度；预训练位置编码器的嵌入信息与图像/文本嵌入信息相当。

Conclusion: GeoSpOT距离可用于指导数据选择、预测模型性能低下区域，即使在下游任务未知或无任务数据时也可进行域外性能估计。

Abstract: In computer vision and machine learning for geographic data, out-of-domain generalization is a pervasive challenge, arising from uneven global data coverage and distribution shifts across geographic regions. Though models are frequently trained in one region and deployed in another, there is no principled method for determining when this cross-region adaptation will be successful. A well-defined notion of distance between distributions can effectively quantify how different a new target domain is compared to the domains used for model training, which in turn could support model training and deployment decisions. In this paper, we propose a strategy for computing distances between geospatial domains that leverages geographic information with Optimal Transport methods (GeoSpOT). In our experiments, GeoSpOT distances emerge as effective predictors of cross-domain transfer difficulty. We further demonstrate that embeddings from pretrained location encoders provide information comparable to image/text embeddings, despite relying solely on longitude-latitude pairs as input. This allows users to get an approximation of out-of-domain performance for geospatial models, even when the exact downstream task is unknown, or no task-specific data is available. Building on these findings, we show that GeoSpOT distances can preemptively guide data selection and enable predictive tools to analyze regions where a model is likely to underperform.

</details>


### [171] [Neuro-Symbolic ODE Discovery with Latent Grammar Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16232)
*Karin Yu,Eleni Chatzi,Georgios Kissas*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本文提出Latent Grammar Flow (LGF)，一种用于从数据中发现常微分方程的神经符号化生成框架。该框架将方程以语法形式表示嵌入离散潜在空间，利用行为损失保持语义相似性，并通过离散流模型指导采样过程生成最优拟合数据的方程，同时支持稳定性等约束条件的嵌入。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 自然系统和工程系统的理解通常依赖于符号化公式（如微分方程），因其具有黑盒模型所不具备的可解释性和可迁移性，因此需要从数据中发现这类符号化表示。

Method: 1. 将方程以语法规则表示嵌入离散潜在空间；2. 使用行为损失函数使语义相似的方程在潜在空间中距离更近；3. 训练离散流模型递归采样生成候选方程；4. 通过条件预测器或规则嵌入引入领域知识和约束。

Result: 框架能够从观测数据中递归生成与数据最拟合的候选常微分方程。

Conclusion: LGF成功将语法表示、离散潜在空间和离散流模型相结合，实现了一种可解释且可转移的神经符号化常微分方程发现框架，同时支持领域知识和约束条件（如稳定性）的嵌入。

Abstract: Understanding natural and engineered systems often relies on symbolic formulations, such as differential equations, which provide interpretability and transferability beyond black-box models.
  We introduce Latent Grammar Flow (LGF), a neuro-symbolic generative framework for discovering ordinary differential equations from data. LGF embeds equations as grammar-based representations into a discrete latent space and forces semantically similar equations to be positioned closer together with a behavioural loss. Then, a discrete flow model guides the sampling process to recursively generate candidate equations that best fit the observed data. Domain knowledge and constraints, such as stability, can be either embedded into the rules or used as conditional predictors.

</details>


### [172] [Enhancing AI and Dynamical Subseasonal Forecasts with Probabilistic Bias Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16238)
*Hannah Guan,Soukayna Mouatadid,Paulo Orenstein,Judah Cohen,Haiyu Dong,Zekun Ni,Jeremy Berman,Genevieve Flaspohler,Alex Lu,Jakob Schloer,Joshua Talib,Jonathan A. Weyn,Lester Mackey*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Decision-makers rely on weather forecasts to plant crops, manage wildfires, allocate water and energy, and prepare for weather extremes. Today, such forecasts enjoy unprecedented accuracy out to two weeks thanks to steady advances in physics-based dynamical models and data-driven artificial intelligence (AI) models. However, model skill drops precipitously at subseasonal timescales (2 - 6 weeks ahead), due to compounding errors and persistent biases. To counter this degradation, we introduce probabilistic bias correction (PBC), a machine learning framework that substantially reduces systematic error by learning to correct historical probabilistic forecasts. When applied to the leading dynamical and AI models from the European Centre for Medium-Range Weather Forecasts (ECMWF), PBC doubles the subseasonal skill of the AI Forecasting System and improves the skill of the operationally-debiased dynamical model for 91% of pressure, 92% of temperature, and 98% of precipitation targets. We designed PBC for operational deployment, and, in ECMWF's 2025 real-time forecasting competition, its global forecasts placed first for all weather variables and lead times, outperforming the dynamical models from six operational forecasting centers, an international dynamical multi-model ensemble, ECMWF's AI Forecasting System, and the forecasting systems of 34 teams worldwide. These probabilistic skill gains translate into more accurate prediction of extreme events and have the potential to improve agricultural planning, energy management, and disaster preparedness in vulnerable communities.

</details>


### [173] [Beyond Distribution Sharpening: The Importance of Task Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16259)
*Sarthak Mittal,Leo Gagnon,Guillaume Lajoie*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本研究探讨了强化学习（RL）在模型训练中的两种范式——分布锐化与任务奖励学习——通过理论分析和实验验证，证明了任务奖励信号能带来更稳定、更优的性能提升，而单纯的分布锐化存在固有局限性和不稳定性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解决一个关键争议：RL训练究竟是真正赋予模型新技能，还是仅通过锐化现有能力分布来激发潜在能力？这对于理解大语言模型能力进化机制至关重要。

Method: 使用RL作为统一框架，实现并对比两种范式：分布锐化（distribution sharpening）和任务奖励学习（task-reward-based learning）。在数学数据集上，使用Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct、Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct和Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507三个模型进行实验验证。

Result: 理论分析揭示了分布锐化的固有局限：从第一性原理出发，证明了其最优解可能不利且方法本质上不稳定。实验结果证实：分布锐化带来的提升有限，而引入任务奖励信号能实现显著的性能改进和稳定学习。

Conclusion: 任务奖励学习优于单纯的分布锐化；RL训练大模型时，需要任务奖励信号才能获得稳健的性能提升；该结论为理解和改进大模型训练策略提供了理论依据和实验支撑。

Abstract: Frontier models have demonstrated exceptional capabilities following the integration of task-reward-based reinforcement learning (RL) into their training pipelines, enabling systems to evolve from pure reasoning models into sophisticated agents. However, debate persists regarding whether RL genuinely instills new skills within a base model or merely sharpens its existing distribution to elicit latent capabilities. To address this dichotomy, we present an explicit comparison between distribution sharpening and task-reward-based learning, utilizing RL as a tool to implement both paradigms. Our analysis reveals the inherent limitations of distribution sharpening, demonstrating from first principles how and why the optima can be unfavorable and the approach fundamentally unstable. Furthermore, our experiments using Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 on math datasets confirm that sharpening yields limited gains, whereas incorporating task-based reward signal can greatly help achieve robust performance improvements and stable learning.

</details>


### [174] [Evaluating the Progression of Large Language Model Capabilities for Small-Molecule Drug Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16279)
*Shriram Chennakesavalu,Kirill Shmilovich,Hayley Weir,Colin Grambow,John Bradshaw,Patricia Suriana,Chen Cheng,Kangway Chuang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本文提出了一个化学导向的任务套件，将分子性质预测、分子表示转换和分子设计任务形式化为强化学习环境，用于评估和后训练大语言模型。实验表明前沿模型在化学任务上表现良好但仍有提升空间，且基于RL的后训练可显著提升性能，使小模型达到与前沿模型相当的水平。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 当前LLM在小分子药物设计中的应用缺乏能反映真实场景的基准测试，导致其实际效用不明确。

Method: 构建了涵盖三类任务（分子性质预测、分子表示转换、分子设计）的化学导向任务套件，并将其形式化为强化学习环境，实现统一的评估和后训练框架。测试了三个模型家族。

Result: 前沿模型在化学任务上日益熟练，但在低数据实验设置下仍有显著改进空间。RL后训练可显著提升性能，较小的后训练模型可与前沿模型竞争。

Conclusion: 通过结合精心设计的评估任务与针对性后训练，可以揭示并弥补LLM在药物发现中的关键能力差距，为LLM在药物发现中的实际应用提供可行路径。

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) have the potential to accelerate small molecule drug design due to their ability to reason about information from diverse sources and formats. However, their practical utility remains unclear due to the lack of benchmarks that reflect real-world scenarios. In this work, we introduce a suite of chemically-grounded tasks spanning molecular property prediction, molecular representation transformations, and molecular design. Importantly, we formulate these tasks as reinforcement learning (RL) environments, enabling a unified approach for evaluation and post-training. Across three model families, we find that frontier models are increasingly proficient at chemical tasks, but that there is significant room for improvement, especially in experimental settings with low data. Critically, we show that RL-based post-training can substantially improve performance. A smaller model post-trained on our environments becomes competitive with state-of-the-art frontier models, despite a significantly weaker base model. This suggests a practical route toward employing LLMs in drug discovery; by combining carefully-designed evaluation tasks with targeted post-training, we can both elucidate and close critical capability gaps.

</details>


### [175] [Geometric regularization of autoencoders via observed stochastic dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16282)
*Sean Hill,Felix X. -F. Ye*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Stochastic dynamical systems with slow or metastable behavior evolve, on long time scales, on an unknown low-dimensional manifold in high-dimensional ambient space. Building a reduced simulator from short-burst ambient ensembles is a long-standing problem: local-chart methods like ATLAS suffer from exponential landmark scaling and per-step reprojection, while autoencoder alternatives leave tangent-bundle geometry poorly constrained, and the errors propagate into the learned drift and diffusion. We observe that the ambient covariance~$Λ$ already encodes coordinate-invariant tangent-space information, its range spanning the tangent bundle. Using this, we construct a tangent-bundle penalty and an inverse-consistency penalty for a three-stage pipeline (chart learning, latent drift, latent diffusion) that learns a single nonlinear chart and the latent SDE. The penalties induce a function-space metric, the $ρ$-metric, strictly weaker than the Sobolev $H^1$ norm yet achieving the same chart-quality generalization rate up to logarithmic factors. For the drift, we derive an encoder-pullback target via Itô's formula on the learned encoder and prove a bias decomposition showing the standard decoder-side formula carries systematic error for any imperfect chart. Under a $W^{2,\infty}$ chart-convergence assumption, chart-level error propagates controllably to weak convergence of the ambient dynamics and to convergence of radial mean first-passage times. Experiments on four surfaces embedded in up to $201$ ambient dimensions reduce radial MFPT error by $50$--$70\%$ under rotation dynamics and achieve the lowest inter-well MFPT error on most surface--transition pairs under metastable Müller--Brown Langevin dynamics, while reducing end-to-end ambient coefficient errors by up to an order of magnitude relative to an unregularized autoencoder.

</details>

### [176] [LLMbench: A Comparative Close Reading Workbench for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15508)
*David M. Berry*

Main category: cs.CY

TL;DR: LLMbench是一款面向数字人文领域的LLM输出对比阅读工具，通过标注面板、概率可视化等方式使生成文本的概率结构在token层级上可见，旨在为生成式AI的批判性研究提供log-probability数据这一尚未充分利用的资源。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing LLM comparison tools (e.g., Google PAIR's LLM Comparator) are designed for quantitative evaluation and user-rating metrics, lacking support for hermeneutic practices and close reading approaches of digital humanities.

Method: LLMbench: a browser-based workbench with side-by-side annotatable panels for comparing two model responses, featuring four analytical overlays (Probabilities, Differences, Tone, Structure) and five analytical modes (Stochastic Variation, Temperature Gradient, Prompt Sensitivity, Token Probabilities, Cross-Model Divergence), with visualizations including heatmaps, entropy sparklines, pixel maps, and 3D probability terrains.

Result: A tool that makes the probabilistic structure of generated text legible at the token level, treating generated text as a research object that emerged from probability distributions, showing the counterfactual history from which each word emerged.

Conclusion: Log-probability data, currently underused in humanistic and social-scientific readings of AI, is an important resource for a critical studies of generative AI models.

Abstract: LLMbench is a browser-based workbench for the comparative close reading of large language model (LLM) outputs. Where existing tools for LLM comparison, such as Google PAIR's LLM Comparator are engineered for quantitative evaluation and user-rating metrics, LLMbench is oriented towards the hermeneutic practices of the digital humanities. Two model responses to the same prompt are side by side in annotatable panels with four analytical overlays (Probabilities for token-level log-probability inspection, Differences for word-level diff across the two panels, Tone for Hyland-style metadiscourse analysis, and Structure for sentence-level parsing with discourse connective highlighting), alongside five analytical modes, Stochastic Variation, Temperature Gradient, Prompt Sensitivity, Token Probabilities, and Cross-Model Divergence, that make the probabilistic structure of generated text legible at the token level. The tool treats the generated text as a research object in its own right from a probability distribution, a text that could have been otherwise, and provides visualisations including continuous heatmaps, entropy sparklines, pixel maps, and three-dimensional probability terrains, that show the counterfactual history from which each word emerged. This paper describes the tool's architecture, its six modes, and its design rationale, and argues that log-probability data, currently underused in humanistic and social-scientific readings of AI, is an important resource for a critical studies of generative AI models.

</details>


### [177] [Can LLMs Help Decentralized Dispute Arbitration? A Case Study of UMA-Resolved Markets on Polymarket](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15674)
*Junhao Wen,Juncen Zhou,Junjie Huang*

Main category: cs.CY

TL;DR: This study evaluates LLMs for Web3 prediction market dispute resolution, finding that while LLMs cannot predict future disputes, they can reproduce UMA's on-chain voting decisions with ~90% accuracy, suggesting potential as an efficient dispute resolution mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Web3 prediction markets face significant consensus disputes (>$972M trading volume affected on Polymarket alone), creating a critical need for objective and efficient dispute resolution mechanisms beyond traditional on-chain voting.

Method: The study evaluated LLM performance in two tasks: (1) retrospectively reproducing UMA's on-chain voting resolutions for disputed events, and (2) prospectively predicting which events would face disputes based on event rules.

Result: Web-enabled LLMs achieved 89.58% agreement with UMA's final resolutions with strong stability, but failed to reliably predict which events would face disputes before they occur.

Conclusion: Web-enabled LLMs cannot reliably predict disputes in advance, but can effectively replicate UMA's on-chain voting decisions with 89.58% accuracy and strong stability once disputes occur.

Abstract: Web3 prediction markets, exemplified by Polymarket, have gained prominence for leveraging collective intelligence to forecast a wide range of social, political, and sports events. However, among the thousands of prediction market events, consensus disputes still arise due to imperfections in market mechanisms. On Polymarket alone, the trading volume involving disputed events has reached $972,370,804.71, underscoring the critical need for objective and efficient dispute resolution. In this study, we introduce large language models (LLMs) to: (1) evaluate whether web-enabled LLMs can reproduce the decision quality of UMA's on-chain voting process once a dispute has been raised, and (2) predict, based on event rules, which market events are likely to face future disputes before they occur. Our findings show that LLMs are unable to reliably predict which events will become disputed in advance; however, once a dispute is initiated, web-enabled LLMs achieve 89.58% agreement with UMA's final resolutions and demonstrate strong stability.

</details>


### [178] [From Vulnerable Data Subjects to Vulnerabilizing Data Practices: Navigating the Protection Paradox in AI-Based Analyses of Platformized Lives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15990)
*Delfina S. Martinez Pandiani,Ella Streefkerk,Laurens Naudts,Paula Helm*

Main category: cs.CY

TL;DR: 本文从静态本质主义视角转向动态实践视角重新审视"脆弱性"概念，聚焦平台化生活语境下数据充裕的伦理挑战。以计算机视觉识别YouTube"家庭博客"中儿童内容的AI案例，揭示"保护悖论"——旨在保护脆弱群体的数据驱动努力反而可能造成新的计算性暴露。文章对AI技术管线进行方法论解构，提出围绕数据集设计、操作化、推理和传播四个关键节点的反思性伦理协议，涵盖暴露、货币化、叙事固化、算法优化四类脆弱化因素。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 传统伦理框架将脆弱性视为数据主体的静态固有属性，难以应对平台化生活中数据充裕的伦理挑战。当前AI for Social Good项目中，研究者面临的伦理困境不仅是"研究谁"，更是"如何通过技术管线将脆弱个体转化为数据主体并进一步使其脆弱化"。

Method: 通过一个具体案例——记者请求使用计算机视觉量化YouTube"家庭视频博客"中儿童内容以推动监管——揭示"保护悖论"现象，并对此AI管线进行方法论解构，识别技术决策中的伦理构成性，进而开发反思性伦理协议。

Result: 识别出四个关键伦理节点（数据集设计、操作化、推理、传播），每个节点存在暴露、货币化、叙事固化、算法优化四类脆弱化风险。协议提供具体引导问题以识别伦理滑移。

Conclusion: AI4SG项目的伦理完整性取决于对技术管线每个环节的批判性审视，而非仅关注研究对象本身。反思性伦理协议可帮助研究者在数据充裕的平台上避免从保护滑向新的提取或暴露。

Abstract: This paper traces a conceptual shift from understanding vulnerability as a static, essentialized property of data subjects to examining how it is actively enacted through data practices. Unlike reflexive ethical frameworks focused on missing or counter-data, we address the condition of abundance inherent to platformized life-a context where a near inexhaustible mass of data points already exists, shifting the ethical challenge to the researcher's choices in operating upon this existing mass. We argue that the ethical integrity of data science depends not just on who is studied, but on how technical pipelines transform "vulnerable" individuals into data subjects whose vulnerability can be further precarized. We develop this argument through an AI for Social Good (AI4SG) case: a journalist's request to use computer vision to quantify child presence in monetized YouTube 'family vlogs' for regulatory advocacy. This case reveals a "protection paradox": how data-driven efforts to protect vulnerable subjects can inadvertently impose new forms of computational exposure, reductionism, and extraction. Using this request as a point of departure, we perform a methodological deconstruction of the AI pipeline to show how granular technical decisions are ethically constitutive. We contribute a reflexive ethics protocol that translates these insights into a reflexive roadmap for research ethics surrounding platformized data subjects. Organized around four critical junctures-dataset design, operationalization, inference, and dissemination-the protocol identifies technical questions and ethical tensions where well-intentioned work can slide into renewed extraction or exposure. For every decision point, the protocol offers specific prompts to navigate four cross-cutting vulnerabilizing factors: exposure, monetization, narrative fixing, and algorithmic optimization. Rather than uncritically...

</details>
